(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) To tobacco law and non-smoking/non-smoking legislation: We all know that smoking is not necessarily healthy; that is incontrovertible. It is equally incontrovertible that all measures to avoid the so-called first cigarette should be treated as positive. However, there is also a need for differentiation, if there is posterity everywhere, in all applications, there are about 600,000 passive smokers who die, and 5 million citizens worldwide are affected by tobacco death. For example, I would have liked to have written that only in Austria the cause of death is number one with 42.7 percent cardiovascular disease – women are even more affected – due to tobacco consumption, and that malignant new formations account for 25.5 percent and respiratory organs are 5%. I would have liked that to be written into applications. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] I would have liked to have written that the 3 million sick days are partly due to tobacco consumption and that most likely the hospital days of nearly 2 million are also attributed to tobacco consumption. Not to talk about the children – you said it, Mrs Glavischnig – who cannot choose where to go with their parents. This situation was unsatisfying for the health politicians. Why? – We all know what happened in Germany when a tobacco law was introduced that provided for a ban on smoking in the gastronomy. Clubs were founded, local owners made an association from their locals. The customer was charged 1 €, which was then credited to him more or less on the invoice. This allowed him to be a club member and this club venue allowed smoking. That was something that the gastronomy who meant it seriously wanted to prevent anyway, because it would have been a circumvention possibility again. I know that gastronomers are very responsible people, and that what Mr Riemer quoted in his initial speech from the letter of a gastronomy, must have put the anger reds in the face of every responsible gastronomy or hotelier, because what he read about what she had to do with controls were two thirds of hygiene checks, off-clause tests, insect protection, cooling chains, all these things. [[Abg. Peter Wurm: It doesn't change reality!]] – That may be unpleasant, but I can’t imagine you being criticized as a customer or responsible host. We have already spoken of the registers on the question: Who likes to pay taxes? - No one. That means you have to let that happen. On the question of worker protection, also often discussed, the colleague Riemer criticised the control of worker protection – registrations, working time records, all these things. That too is something that makes for worker protection. And of course these are also smoke-free jobs for people in the gastronomy. If you look at the story of the ban on smoking, starting from 1992, when Minister Ausserwinkler started to take these steps, I think we have taken a big step to stop people from smoking. My daughter has recently sent me an entry that I wrote to her in her main book. He said: What benefits the best education – children are after all. – And so is it! Children who see their parents smoking, children who see people smoking who can smoke everywhere, see smoking much more relaxed than just saying: not here! In prevention, that's right, there's still a lot to do. The first step, I believe, has already been taken. We have launched a campaign entitled “YOLO – You Only Live Once”, which aims exactly at what Mrs. Deputies Dietrich said. With this campaign you go into schools, it will start very massively at the start of school. In schools it is explained how harmful smoking is, with various materials such as quiz, with all these things – a campaign made by young people for young people. Whatever we will do – the law is a step, I have already said it is a multifactorial story. With a law that prohibits smoking alone in the gastronomy, people will not be stopped from smoking, but with preventive measures and also with the possibility of getting out of smoking. That is why we will continue to try to make it easier for people to get out of smoking. We will have several more initiatives. You'll see, we'll try some more. I think this is a good day. I am really glad that I succeeded in negotiating this in the short time with the coalition partner. I would also like to thank Mr. Vice Chancellor Mitterlehner, with whom it was very, very constructive to negotiate. My thanks also to the gastronomy, to the Chamber of Commerce, to the hotel industry with which we have had many discussions, have been very good, very controversial discussions, and have come to this compromise. I think it is a good step in matters of non-smokers protection and a good step that perhaps fewer people will die from the consequences of tobacco consumption – the one they smoke themselves or the one they passively smoke.

With a look at the figures, we can see that in Austria about 14,000 people die each year from the consequences of active and passive tobacco consumption. I think that policy is also required, and with this law we are setting up a corresponding measure. In doing this, we are particularly encouraged by the fact that over 63 percent of the Austrian population support this non-smokers protection. With non-smokers protection, we meet this desire, including those of the workers, of the Zig thousands of employees in the gastronomy, for which we create better working conditions. For us, it is a premise to say that personal sensitivity must come back when it comes to other people's interests to protecting the other person in the field of health. With this rule of law, we also make it possible to meet those companies that have already invested in non-smokers protection under the old law by providing them with an early depreciation of 30 percent in addition to these depreciation possibilities. In advance, the obstacles on the way to this tobacco law were already addressed, so you can only be congratulated, Mrs. Federal Minister – it was not easy! – but I would also like to mention Mr Vice-Chancellor, because he has finally put himself on your side and made the ban on smoking possible in the coalition. A colleague – I don't see him now – meant he doesn't know where the trip is going: For me, the journey goes there that this beautiful Austria becomes non-smoking and healthier. – In this sense, I am glad that we are adopting this law today.

One is the abolition of the blood donation ban due to sexual orientation. This is mainly about the risk of infection and the risk of infection in relation to HIV. De facto has caused homosexual men to be excluded from blood donation and that, for example, on the Red Cross website, the passage is still to be read that men who had sex with men should not donate blood. There is now an ECJ judgment, which clearly states: Discrimination and exclusion from blood donation due to sexual orientation is not allowed. All alternatives must be examined before, for example, a very detailed questionnaire must be filled out and all possible test methods applied, and only if it turns out that risk behaviour is not excluded can blood donation be banned. I think it was high time. We have already talked about the content of this proposal several times. In the end, I find it unacceptable that there is still such a provision in a country like Austria. I understand very well that homosexual men feel discriminated against by this and are very hurt that there is still this provision. The investigations often take place under humiliating and discriminatory circumstances, it is really an unreasonable state. I don't think you can promote that. We should go about making voluntary, anonymous, free, low-sleep and high-quality offers, so that sex workers are really helped. It is a genetic engineering amendment or a GMO-free Austria, based on EU Directive 2015/412, which allows Member States to restrict or prohibit the cultivation of genetically modified organisms, GMOs, in their territory, in whole or in part, by the Minister of Health and the Federal Minister for Agriculture and Forestry. Both proposals attempt to regulate the authorisation and cultivation of GMO seeds by two ministries and nine national laws. This is, of course, a complex and difficult task, as we will see then. Since, of course, genetic engineering can cause immediate and immediate damage to human health, it is the responsibility of health care, and with regard to the objective of protecting human health and its offspring, the Federal Ministry of Health has prepared and implemented the management. For one thing, genetic engineering is the pandora's canals and is discussed again and again controversially. But we must distinguish: Is this about agricultural or medical matters? – In medicine, on the other hand, today’s insulin or contributions to cancer therapy are components, or one may need the production of enzymes or fine chemicals for industrial purposes in microbiology or environmental protection technology. It is therefore an issue that will be difficult for us not only today but also in the future. This also has to do with the world hunger to be fought by the agricultural industry, by using genetically modified organisms by the agricultural groups, while at the same time providing the corresponding plant protection products for these hybrid crops and thus ensuring the dependence of farmers in the First, Second and Third Worlds. Of course, the statement of Jean-Claude Juncker shows how difficult this is. Of course, it doesn't always make me very happy with his statement, which reads: “I also intend to review the legislation on the authorisation of genetically modified organisms. In my view, it is simply not right for the Commission to be legally obliged to approve the import and processing of new organisms, even though a clear majority of the Member States are opposed. The Commission should be able to give the majority of democratically elected governments at least the same weight as scientific opinions, especially when it comes to the safety of our food () – food is not genetically modified, it is no longer food, but food means that it is reproducible again. As you can see, a rather interesting, exciting matter. Let us remember that this is a billion-dollar business, including the Group's future, and how Austria will be able to behave accordingly – whether we can cope with this pressure. This is a question mark and will certainly not be the last point we are discussing. ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e11587	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! We are very pleased that, after long, difficult negotiations at EU level, it has succeeded in establishing a right, namely on the cultivation of genetically modified organisms, say plants, nationally. Austria is therefore still self-determined in this area and will develop national strategies for further safeguarding the freedom of genetic engineering in our country. That is, we have fought the right that if an EU-wide permit for the cultivation of genetically modified organisms, say plants, comes, our territory can be excluded or the cultivation can be prevented. Under the leadership of the Minister of Health or the Ministry of Health, we have also successfully fought for this right. Two laws – and I come to the competences – that is, the genetic engineering law as well as the genetic engineering ban-framework law, should continue to prevent the cultivation of genetically modified organisms. At national level In addition, there is the report of the Commission on Genetic Engineering, which is presented to the National Council every three years. However, this report does not only include plants, but generally the whole area of genetic engineering. There are also contributions from the Ministry of Science. But back to the laws: The technical implementation of the laws includes two phases. Phase 1 precisely regulates the genetic engineering law, says the exception of the state territory from the authorisation of cultivation of genetically modified organisms, say plants. This competence – as the competence question was addressed – is in the area of our Minister for Health Sabine Oberhauser. Phase 2 means coordination for the rejection of cultivation, although a group nevertheless insists on cultivation in the field of state territory or countries. This is the subject of the Gegentechnik-Aufbauverbots-Rahmengesetz as well as the Variety Protection Act, where the competence lies with the Federal Minister Rupprechter. A separate set-up advisory board should ensure the future assurance and coordination of the Austrian strategy on this important issue, both for the Federal Government and for the countries. In addition to the country representatives, this advisory board will include many representatives of all the areas concerned, including the Agency for Health and Food Safety as well as representatives of NGOs and many more. Experts from the area of the Chamber of Workers are also being taught to be able to bring in their expertise. Finally, all this was only possible because the coalition with the involvement of the Greens – I thank Mr Pirklhuber – has decided to join together for a national shoulder closure on the topic of “gentech-free” in Austria. A thank you at this point to you, Mrs. Federal Minister, that the Genetic Engineering Act could be presented for decision-making so quickly.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e11761	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Steinbichler.]] If we are implementing the EU directive today, this is really a great political success for Austria. There is an EU-wide authorisation procedure for genetically modified varieties, but we have a right to self-determination within the framework of EU legislation, i.e. a state, a region can declare itself free of genetic engineering. We have succeeded, and this has been a long-term hard fight. How is it at the European level? – There is an authorisation procedure for the genetically modified varieties of maize, soybean and rape, and they must then be applied throughout Europe. Austria has established these approved varieties with a national ban on cultivation. Only so far has it been the case that the European Commission has repeatedly applied to tip these national, Austrian crop bans, and so in March 2009, I was Environment Minister at that time. After a hard fight, we succeeded in defending this ban on cultivation. I thought that could not be so if Austria was an ordinary EU member, a state that integrates into the EU system, which does not want us to use genetic engineering. We have launched an initiative for a right of self-determination on the issue of genetic engineering. At the time, we were supported by the Netherlands and our Eastern European neighbours, and some ten countries supported Austria in the initiative. The large EU states have had concerns about the WTO's contradictions, and other states have said that is disturbing the internal market. We have said that is not the case, we want to apply subsidiarity, a nation-state can make this possible within the framework of EU legislation. [[Abg. Neubauer: That's where he wants self-determination! But not at South Tyrol!]] We have fought, and then the great political success that the former President of the European Commission, Barroso, said: Yes, there should be a right of self-determination in the matter of genetic engineering. If a region – not only in Austria, but also in other countries – does not want to do so, it should have the opportunity to do so. Under the Danish EU Presidency, a proposal has then been drawn up that has already been supported by 21 states in the EU. Only the large countries were opposed, which have really feared market disturbances. Finally, Austria also managed to secure this right of self-determination in conjunction with the European Parliament. And now we are here to implement it nationally. I think this is a great political success. Because do you know what today's decision shows? That a small state like Austria can also shape the common Europe. It is not as always said that in Europe we say yes and Amen for all things. Not right here! We have achieved a success against the toughest resistance. We could do it because we worked solidly because we remained consistent and because we were also stubborn enough to implement it. We've cleared up all our concerns. I'm not one of those who say that genetic engineering is a cure. Many say that we can secure world nutrition. I don't think so, because: Farmers come into a new dependence on seeds. The second point: You often have to buy plant protection products in combination with them. The third point: We see that in countries, for example in South America, where genetic engineering is applied, nature is actually stronger than all technological applications, that there are no longer genetically modified varieties that show this effect and that the other plants develop resistance to plant protection products. That means genetic engineering is not the answer. If we implement these laws today, we will already be able to achieve a national epaulment so that we can remain free from genetic engineering. Finally, I also see an advantage in that we can produce high-quality GMO-free foods and claim to be on the world market as well as on the European market. Thank you very much for your approval.

This chain, as one can imagine, is faulty and leads to a mass storage of data without concrete, well-founded suspicions. Under this law, social fraud can be understood pretty much because the law is not limited to criminal offences under the criminal code. On the contrary: Through the back door, in the explanations of the law, the concept of social fraud is so widely interpreted that it is only quite possible to speak of a mass storage, of a storage of data. The fact that the storage of these data can be started without the public prosecutor's consent is self-evident. This is therefore carried out by persons who are not necessarily qualified to be able to assess criminally based criminal offences. Worse enough that these circumstances are highly questionable by the rule of law, but the data will be stored for another five years without having to be determined. That is an expression of a data collection that we also have in other places in this Republic, keyword surveillance state at this point. This is, of course, an expression of convenience, because it is easier to collect this data for the time being, rather than to find out later specifically for proper suspicions. Second point: This government proposal is also being discussed by NEOS. We finally want to create legal certainty when, in the framework of GPLA procedures, qualifications are made from self-employed to self-employed. In these requalifications, self-employed are made employees, against their will, although they pay their contributions properly. There is no proper consultation procedure, there are no conciliation bodies that decide in these cases. The purpose of this proposal is to provide legal certainty. A framework is to be set when it really is self-employment. Of course, our proposal does not ignore the problem of disinheritance, and it will also prevent people from being pushed into precarious employment by disinheritance. This legal certainty is also important in relation to the above mentioned social fraud control, because the law also mentions the high residue of social security funds as a reference for entrepreneurship. This is, of course, a consequence of this requalification, resulting in this backwardness. In the course of the new risk analysis, the data from the GPLA procedures should help to qualify for imagers more quickly. In this connection, however, legal uncertainty in requalifications will lead to conspicuousness and fair companies are put under the suspicion of social fraud. Such misunderstandings – once euphemistically formulated – must by no means happen, and on the other hand we will resist vehemently.

Point Report of the Committee on Labour and Social Affairs on the Government template (697, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Act, which amends a Federal Law on Alternative Dispute Resolution in Consumer Matters and amends the Consumer Protection Act, the Fees Act 1957 and the Consumer Authority Cooperation Act (772 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e16483	We're going to the 18th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First of all, Mrs Lueger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e16510	Madam President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen in the House! This bill concerns the implementation of an EU directive and at the same time an EU regulation, which is called a little more wooden alternative dispute resolution law. I believe that this is an important point for consumers, and the directive requires that it be implemented in all Member States in order to create a comprehensive network for dispute resolution in consumer business. For this purpose, we have eight referee bodies, and this law requires the quality criteria to be met. This Alternative Dispute Resolution Act allows a simple, efficient, ultimately cost-effective, low-density and quick alternative to court proceedings and is intended to help in domestic or cross-border disputes. Although we have a well-functioning trial – yes, that’s true –, but if we look at the relationship between the actual dispute value and the amount of court costs when people go to court – the dispute value is small, the very high court costs are compared to that – then this alternative dispute resolution law is a very good alternative so that people do not have to go to court. Consumers are free to complain about purchasing and service contracts, and it is also a voluntary matter for companies to participate in these procedures. I think, however, that it is quite simply a good opportunity for entrepreneurs instead of making lengthy court proceedings a trust-building measure towards customers and creating customer commitment through a quick and efficient agreement. We also discussed in the committee why there are no longer any dispute resolution bodies. We have agreed to take care of each other; everything else would be an unnecessary wild growth, and one would also like the same criteria to apply to all and then they are certified. That is why we have decided on this eight – I would like to mention it briefly: these are the conciliation body of the E-Control, the Telekom Conciliation Body and the RTR's Post Conciliation Office, the Passenger and Passenger Rights Agency, the Joint Conciliation Body of the Austrian credit industry, the Internet Ombudsman, the Preparatory Office and the Conciliation Point for Consumer Business, which is ultimately such a collection point, where all other things that are not to be allocated in advance are dealt with. The rules of procedure laid down by the law are information requirements to be fulfilled by companies and, ultimately, there is also clarification of public authorities and reporting obligations. Most of these schemes are expected to be applicable with January of next year. You didn't rely on it and you said you'd just try it out, but there was a big pilot project that was carried out by the BMASK. The conciliation procedures themselves are divided into three phases, namely the negotiation, mediation and conciliation, and finally it has been shown that many applications could be resolved in these procedures; in particular, when entrepreneurs volunteered, 57 percent of cases have come to a solution. I am convinced that this is a good way for consumers, a good way for consumer protection, and I hope for broad approval.

It is still very open to what extent this small contribution would have to be made. On the one hand, this is very positive from a socio-political point of view, because a dispute value of €100 for many Austrian citizens is de facto a lot of money, but it is therefore necessary to see how this small contribution should even look. Another questionable point from our point of view is the definition of the upper and lower limit. It is then crucial whether judicial enforcement is economically meaningful or not or whether it is financially viable at all or not. The third point is voluntary participation in the procedure. [[18:06:01]] 26. Point Report of the Committee on Agriculture and Forestry on the Government template (673d.b._Other Organiser Federal Law on the Prohibition of the cultivation of genetically modified organisms (Gentechnik-Prohibition Framework Act) and amending the Variety Protection Act (764, e.g.)
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19360	Now we come to the 26th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Auer. — (DE) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to speak on behalf of the Group of the European - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19389	Mr President. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The GMO, based on the Directive of the European Union, is an EU legal basis for the adoption of bans on cultivation. As a result, Austria's required right of self-determination is enshrined in European law. Austria can decide at EU level, at national level, whether or not genetically modified seed is grown. There was a struggle for this right of self-determination – one could almost say – for decades, and it has ultimately succeeded in deciding nationally what is being grown in our fields. Austria was a pioneer. To be quite precise: Upper Austria was the pioneer in Austria. This was an important goal, a signal for Europe, for many regions. I do not know that there has been a very constructive cooperation between the individual clubs over the last few days, with Mr Preiner, in particular Mr Pirklhuber, for which I would like to express my express thanks. In turn, one sees that it is quite possible to bring a reasonable thing to a successful conclusion across party borders. I can also expressly say that Mr Schellhorn has signaled to go along. I also invite the other parties to support this request. Therefore, in order to implement what we have agreed, I am also submitting an amendment which has already been distributed. The reason for this amendment – or rather the core – is Abs. 5 to 7 of § 3. Abs. 5: The Federal Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry has to prohibit cultivation if reasons for Abs. 2 and 3. In Abs. 6: In the case of prohibition of one of the articles. 5 existing grounds can be used for any other GMO species authorised in the EU. In the event of a ban on cultivation, the implementation of national legislation shall be subject to the conditions laid down in national law. Wide-ranging expertise will be strengthened and strengthened by the use of experts from the seed industry, the eco-office, the environmental roof association and the ARGE genetic engineering community. The Federal Minister for Agriculture and Forestry may also prohibit cultivation if the reasons for a ban exist in all the federal states and the advisory board agrees to this prohibition. The countries are responsible for enforcement. This government template and amendment proposal is therefore a compromise between purely federal competence and purely national competence. Ladies and gentlemen, this amendment has been explained in the key points. I say: I am pleased that it was possible to find a broad base. I should like to thank you once again for your approval. Another comment: We told the Austrian dairy farmers in Austria – although this has nothing to do with the ban on cultivation and so on – at the time, they may use non-genotechnic soya, and these additional costs would be paid to them. Unfortunately, this is not the case. We are committed to a corresponding wide-ranging non-genotechnic feeding, we also demand such a feeding, and we have thus incurred higher costs for farmers, but the truth is that the commitments of trade and those marketing organisations have not been respected. Originally, there was the promise: 3 cents, 2 cents, 1 cents, it has become less and less, and we note today that some farmers – especially from Upper Austria – deliver to another country, where, although they do not have these costs, although these conditions are not required to receive more for the litre or the kilo of milk than in their own country, where the pre-costs are higher. The same happens to us in the pigmeat sector. Ladies and gentlemen, in the long run it will not be possible to bear more costs and get worse prices. But it is a very positive day – coming back to this request – because it is possible in the future to make it clear that a national ban on cultivation can be implemented. Thanks again to all the colleagues who go along, and nevertheless the invitation to all those who might think about it at the last minute that it would be a positive signal for Austria's fields, for Austrian agriculture and for the Austrian leadership in this area.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19462	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian The amendment which has just been submitted, explained in its main guidelines and which is currently being distributed, is sufficiently supported and is under negotiation. The application reads as follows: [[Quoted print version removed]]
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19604	Next speaker: Mr Preiner. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19625	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Today, we discuss and decide on the so-called GMO cultivation ban law, which provides the basis for the GMO cultivation ban to continue to exist in Austria in the future. The aim of the law is to maintain biodiversity and farming in Austria. I am very grateful to the coalition partner, Mr Auer, but also to the representatives of the two opposition parties, Mr Pirklhuber and Mr Schellhorn, for their constructive involvement in the talks. I hope that the two other opposition parties will also be in favour of the law in current debate and also of voting behaviour. Austria has already known itself five years ago, 2010, in the sense of the right to self-determination on GMO-free farming in the agricultural sector. This EU Directive 2015/412 also sets out at EU level the possibility of enabling GMO-free cultivation in all EU Member States. The new law we are going to pass today has two stages. One step concerns measures against GMOs in the Ministry of Health. In this respect, the draft law on genetic engineering has already been adopted this morning. The second stage concerns the Ministry of Agriculture. This step will then enter into force if, in the context of an authorisation procedure, groups still grow GMOs in Austria. Have a rich eye on it – whether through a product or through entire product groups. Then the Ministry of Health may approach the Ministry of Agriculture and the Environment in good time, even in good time, to a advisory board to be drawn up, in which country representatives are also represented with a seat and a vote to enforce GMO freedom according to qualified arguments in Austria. What arguments are these? – Environmental measures and objectives, but also considerations on the part of spatial planning and on land use. These are just a few examples. I would like to remind you of this: If there is clarity in the advisory board, the Ministry of Agriculture, the responsible Minister for the Environment and Agriculture, is invited to adopt a corresponding ban at the overall federal level by regulation. That, I think, is the core piece. Why is this procedure necessary? – Quite clearly: Because the countries are responsible for the cultivation and control of agricultural products. The Federal Government is subject to the authorisation and marketing of agricultural products. We hope to be on a good path with the new law in the future, so that Austria not only remains GMO-free in cultivation, but also that the use of feed may be GMO-free in the future. Previously, we heard from colleagues Auer the example of soy use. This is a warm thanks also to the Minister of Agriculture and the Environment, to the experts at the Ministry. I hope that we will not remain the only ones in the EU, rigorously implementing this EU directive. ParlaMint-AT_2015-07-08-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19695	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The previous speakers have already explained in detail what this law is all about. At the beginning, perhaps I should like to stress the importance: In fact, the decision-making of this present-day GMO framework law is a clear commitment to the further development of an ecological and genetic-free agriculture in Austria. That's the basis for that. In the meantime, this is the challenge: We need the protection of rural agriculture, but also nature, biodiversity. If you imagine that Monsanto, the largest agricultural group in the genetic engineering business, is currently in the process, Syngenta, one of the largest European chemical and agricultural groups to take over €45 billion, then you know what it's all about. It's about big business, and if we don't get federal unity. We will then move the shorter towards such groups and stakeholders. I would also like to say this very clearly to the FPÖ address. Why? – Because it includes competences from both areas. This is an appeal to all Members of Parliament, in their region, to promote this common strategy in their own country, and not to a strategy that goes against each other. That is, in order to clarify this again, the competences for seeds are federal competence: authorisation, recognition, marketing of seed. Once a seed is authorised as a variety and then it is about cultivation, of course it is a matter of country. The actual cut question and cut amount exists when it is forbidden if it is not grown yet. There is a long way to fight at the legal level, who is drawing the shorter. Ladies and gentlemen, we have therefore found a good compromise. This law has a role to play, and that is what it is supposed to be for Europe as a whole. We are the first country to implement this possibility of self-determination in this way. We've been fighting for over 15 years. I would like to remind you once again: We have jointly decided on the right of self-determination in 2008 in the National Council in a joint resolution on green initiative for the first time. We have reaffirmed this several times, and the ministers Berlakovich and Rupprechter have correctly represented it at European level, and it has been greatly improved with the support of the European Parliament – and I would like to stress this once again. Now it's about implementing it optimally. There needs to be another shoulder closure, and we have done so in an intensive, good negotiation – and I would like to thank you for it – in deep discussions and implemented it. What's this about? . We have thus made national bans on cultivation possible in cooperation with the countries. We have optimally involved the federal states in decision-making and also in strategy development, and that is, please, an appreciation: This is a strategy in which the federal states are involved. We have also involved environmental organisations and stakeholders, from the Chamber of Agriculture and the Chamber of Workers to the Federal Environment Agency in decision-making and consultation. Finally, we have also provided for group bans on GMOs, i.e. simplified procedures to actually prevent several genetic engineering plants in one process. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a really important step and, as I said, it can have a model effect. I would now like to refer to a joint proposal which we are submitting and which I would like to briefly explain. I therefore make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] The matter is actually very complex, I am saving myself the explanations here. It is simply an objective examination of the ways in which seed is placed on the market, how it can be protected from contamination. We also want to deal with this new advisory board, which we set up with this law, and so to speak, give it the first small job assignment. Ladies and gentlemen, let me summarize: It is a good day for a free genetic engineering Austria and a good day for Europe. We are implementing a European directive here, we are pioneers in this area. And my appeal from this point on to the Federal Council again: He is supposed to follow our decision that it would be nice if we could decide on this in July in the Federal Council. - Thank you.

Many of us are not aware of what it means for deer or red-wild populations, of snowshoe hikers who are away from the required routes, to be rushed out of their winter camp, to have to flee through deep snow, and to have to burn vital body substance. Such processes often lead to the death of the wild and are often caused by extreme biting situations. The report should therefore also serve to make all of us aware that, as legislators, we also have responsibility in this area to maintain the necessary resting space for the wild – especially when the reputation for further openings of the forest comes from various sides. Excellent examples of proper behaviour in nature and forest should be of particular importance in the report, such as the initiative of the Austrian Federal Forest Fair Play, in which the right behaviour is clearly listed in the forest, or the initiative “Respect Your Borders” of the Vorarlberg State Government, which, by signposting in the ski area, draws attention to not leaving the secure ski routes in order not to damage massively through off-road activities in rest areas of the Wild Forest and Wild. Awareness-building of the tourists and also our people seeking recreation is not necessary; and in addition, ladies and gentlemen, we should also use the game-damage report, which will be published and discussed annually in the future. This report should not be a plump of reference to the hunting, but rather an indication of shortcomings, a assistance where necessary, and an awareness-building on a broad front should be the objectives of this report, which we would like to discuss with you on this basis.

Then: Although declared and marked, the fact is that biocidal products can cause serious damage in nature, in the environment and ultimately in all of us. As in my last speech on this subject, I would like to say again today that, in the whole legal framework of biocidal products and chemicals, we must not forget that, in particular, the smallest but very important species such as bees suffer from products of this kind and a real bee death is recognizable. Our task is really to face this bee death as soon as possible. We must do everything we can to protect our environment in order to keep our regions alive, even for small inhabitants, like bees. It is important to decide on this law. But just as important is the protection of our environment. That is why it is also my concern today to stress what has been done. Einstein said: “When the bees disappear, man has only four years to live.” Four years, a little more or less, that means for our environment, for my region in Salzburg definitely: When the bees die, the whole region dies! That this is not happening, we must stand up here in the House. That is why it is good for us to adopt these laws. But it is equally good and important to limit the abundance of these products and to look forward to protecting our smallest inhabitants, the bees of this earth, which have such an important function for us. Thank you.

I ask those ladies and gentlemen, who are also at third reading for this draft law, to have a sign of approval. - That's the majority. The draft law is therefore also adopted at third reading.
